w_{+}=\frac{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}{1+\theta}\mathrm{Re}(\widetilde{\omega}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}),\quad w_{-}=\frac{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}{1-\theta}\mathrm{Re}(\widetilde{\omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}})\,.

(4.6)

Taking the differentials of
\omega
,

\widetilde{\omega}
with respect to
w_{+}
,
w_{-}
in
(
4.3
), (
4.5
), for any
fixed
\theta
, we get

\mathrm{d}\omega\wedge\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}}\mathrm{d}w_{+}\wedge\mathrm{d}w_{-}\,,

(4.7)

and we can view this as a relation between differential forms on

\Gamma_{\theta}
.

As for
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
in (
3
) we have no

\widetilde{\zeta}
and we choose the simpler decomposition

\zeta=z_{+}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}+z_{-}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}},

(4.8)

z_{+}=\mathrm{Re}(\zeta\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}),\quad z_{-}=\mathrm{Re}(\zeta\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}})\,.

(4.9)

The first term
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)
in (
3
) becomes

\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\hskip-7.11317pt\int\frac{a\chi\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi}\mathrm{d}w_{+}\wedge\mathrm{d}w_{-}}{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}(z_{+}-\frac{1-\theta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}w_{+})+\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}(z_{-}-\frac{1+\theta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}w_{-})}

(4.10)

with
\theta=1-\delta
.

For
\zeta
in a fixed bounded set in
\mathbb{C}
or equivalently
for
(z_{+},z_{-})
in a fixed bounded set in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)=&{\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{F}{h}}\hskip-3.0pt\int\hskip-7.11317pt\int\frac{|\chi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})|\mathrm{d}w_{+}\mathrm{d}w_{-}}{|z_{+}-\frac{1-\theta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}w_{+}|+|z_{-}-\frac{1+\theta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}w_{-}|}\,,\\
&\hbox{ where }F:=\sup_{\Gamma_{\theta}}(-\mathrm{Im}\,\phi)\,.\end{split}

(4.11)

Here
w_{\pm}={\mathcal{O}}(1)
on
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi
by (
4.4
), (
4.6
) so the last integral is

{\mathcal{O}}(1)\int_{J}\Big(1+\big|\ln|z_{+}-\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}w_{+}|\big|\Big)\,\mathrm{d}w_{+}\,,

(4.12)

where
J
is a fixed bounded interval, independent of

z_{\pm}
and
\theta=1-\delta
. This can be written

{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\int_{\widetilde{J}_{\delta}}\Big(1+\big|\ln|z_{+}-\widetilde{w}_{+}|\big|\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{w}_{+}\,,

(4.13)

where

\widetilde{J}_{\delta}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{2(1+\theta^{2})}}\,J\,.

(4.14)

Here
\widetilde{J}_{\delta}
is an interval of length
{\mathcal{O}}(\delta)
. If
\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta})\geq\delta
, we can estimate the expression (
4.13
) by

\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\left(1+|\ln(\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta}))|\right).

If
\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta})<\delta
, we
compare with the “worst” case when
z_{+}
is the midpoint of

\widetilde{J}_{\delta}
and estimate (
4.13
) by

\leq\frac{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}{\delta}\int_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}}(\delta)}\big(1+|\ln t|\big)\,\mathrm{d}t=\frac{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}{\delta}\Big[2t-t\ln t\Big]_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}}(\delta)}\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\ln\Big(\frac{1}{\delta}\Big)\,.

In conclusion,

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)|\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{F}{h}}\times\begin{cases}\left(1+|\ln(\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta}))|\right),\hbox{ when }\,\,\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta})\geq\delta\,,\\
\ln(\frac{1}{\delta}),\hbox{ when }\,\,\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{+},\widetilde{J}_{\delta})\leq\delta\,.\end{cases}

(4.15)

This gives

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)|\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{F}{h}}\big(1+|\ln(|z_{+}|+\delta)|\big)\,.

(4.16)

We next consider
\underset{\delta\to 0}{\lim}\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)
. Notice that when
\delta=0
, (
4.3
), (
4.5
) reduce to

\omega=w_{-}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}},\quad\widetilde{\omega}=w_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\,.

(4.17)

When
z_{+}\neq 0
, we can let
\theta\to 1
(
\delta\to 0
) in
(
4.10
) and get

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)=\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\hskip-7.11317pt\int\frac{a\chi\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\phi}{h}}}{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}z_{+}+\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}(z_{-}-w_{-})}\,\mathrm{d}w_{+}\mathrm{d}w_{-}\,.

(4.18)

We sum up the results so far without using the coordinates

w_{\pm}
,
z_{\pm}
. From (
4.8
) we get

|z_{+}|=\mathrm{dist\,}(\zeta,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R})\,.

(4.19)

Proposition 4.2
.

Define
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)
as in Proposition
3.5

and let
\zeta
vary in a compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
. With
F
as
in (
4.11
) we have

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)|\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{F}{h}}\left(1+|\ln(\mathrm{dist\,}(\zeta,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R})+\delta)|\right).

(4.20)

When
\zeta\in K\setminus(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R})
,

\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int\hskip-7.11317pt\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\frac{1}{\omega-\zeta}\,(a\chi)(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\wedge\mathrm{d}\omega}{2\mathrm{i}}

(4.21)

is finite and

\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1-\delta)\longrightarrow\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1),\quad\delta\to 0\,.

(4.22)

We now assume that
\phi
is holomorphic in

\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2})
(either in
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\xi,\eta}
or in
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\omega,\tilde{\omega}}
, depending on the
which coordinates we use). Assume that

\phi(0,0)=0,\ \ \nabla\phi(0,0)=0.

(4.23)

Let
\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)
be the 2nd order Taylor polynomial of
\phi
at
(0,0)
,

\phi(\xi,\eta)=\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)+{\mathcal{O}}(|(\xi,\eta)|^{3})\,.

(4.24)

We use the same notations for functions in the variables
\omega,\widetilde{\omega}
,

\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})=\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})+{\mathcal{O}}(|(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})|^{3})\,.

(4.25)

Assume that
\phi_{2}
is real-valued on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\xi,\eta}
,
hence

\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{\lambda}{2}\xi^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}\eta^{2}+\rho\,\xi\eta,\ \ \lambda,\mu,\rho\in\mathbb{R}\,.

(4.26)

Assume that

\lambda,\,\mu>0.

(4.27)

Then

\det\phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}=\det\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&\rho\\
\rho&-\mu\end{pmatrix}=-(\lambda\mu+\rho^{2})<0\,,

(4.28)

so the quadratic form
\phi_{2}
is non-degenerate of signature
(+,-)
.
Conversely, if
\phi_{2}
is a real quadratic form which is
non-degenerate of signature
(+,-)
, then we have (
4.26
) and

\lambda\mu+\rho^{2}>0
. Of course, in this case we can perform a rotation
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
so that we get (
4.26
) in the new coordinates
with
\rho=0
,
\lambda,\,\mu>0
.

Recall that
\Gamma_{\theta}
is given by (
3.18
),

\xi=(1+\mathrm{i}\theta)t,\quad\eta=(1-\mathrm{i}\theta)s,\quad t,s\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(4.29)

so that

|\xi|^{2}=(1+\theta^{2})t^{2},\quad|\eta|^{2}=(1+\theta^{2})s^{2}\hbox{ on
}\Gamma_{\theta}\,.

We have

\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{\lambda}{2}(1+\mathrm{i}\theta)^{2}t^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}(1-\mathrm{i}\theta)^{2}s^{2}+\rho(1+\theta^{2})ts\hbox{ on }\Gamma_{\theta}\,.

(4.30)

In particular,

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)=\theta(\lambda t^{2}+\mu s^{2}),\ \ (\xi,\eta)\in\Gamma_{\theta}

(4.31)

and from (
4.27
) we conclude that

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{\theta}{1+\theta^{2}}(\lambda|\xi|^{2}+\mu|\eta|^{2})\asymp\theta(|\xi|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})\hbox{ on
}\Gamma_{\theta}\,.

(4.32)

We now switch to the variables
\omega,\widetilde{\omega}
. Since

\xi=\frac{1}{2}(\omega+\widetilde{\omega})\,,\quad\eta=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}(\omega-\widetilde{\omega})\,,

(4.33)

we get from (
4.26
),

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})=\frac{\lambda}{8}(\omega+\widetilde{\omega})^{2}+\frac{\mu}{8}(\widetilde{\omega}-\omega)^{2}-\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{4}(\omega+\widetilde{\omega})(\omega-\widetilde{\omega})\,.

(4.34)

It follows that

\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\phi_{2}=\left(\frac{\mu+\lambda}{4}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{2}\right)\widetilde{\omega}-\frac{\mu-\lambda}{4}\omega\,,

(4.35)

so
\mathbb{C}\ni\widetilde{\omega}\longmapsto\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})
has the unique and non-degenerate critical point

\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega)=\frac{\mu-\lambda}{\mu+\lambda+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\,\omega\,.

(4.36)

We have

\omega+\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega)=\frac{2(\mu+\mathrm{i}\rho)}{\mu+\lambda+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\omega\,,

(4.37)

\omega-\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega)=\frac{2(\lambda+\mathrm{i}\rho)}{\mu+\lambda+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\omega\,.

(4.38)

Using this in (
4.34
), we get

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))=\frac{\frac{\lambda}{2}(\mu+\mathrm{i}\rho)^{2}+\frac{\mu}{2}(\lambda+\mathrm{i}\rho)^{2}-\mathrm{i}\rho(\mu+\mathrm{i}\rho)(\lambda+\mathrm{i}\rho)}{\Big((\mu+\mathrm{i}\rho)+(\lambda+\mathrm{i}\rho)\Big)^{2}}\,\omega^{2}\,,

which simplifies to

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))=\frac{\lambda\mu+\rho^{2}}{2(\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)}\,\omega^{2}\,.

(4.39)

When
\rho=0
this gives

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))=\frac{\lambda\mu}{2(\mu+\lambda)}\,\omega^{2}\,,

(4.40)

and when
\lambda=\mu
,

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))=\frac{\lambda+\mu-\mathrm{i}2\rho}{8}\,\omega^{2}=\frac{\lambda-\mathrm{i}\rho}{4}\,\omega^{2}\,.

(4.41)

By (
4.35
),

\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}^{2}\phi_{2}=\frac{\mu+\lambda}{4}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{2}\,,

(4.42)

hence,

\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\mu+\lambda}{4}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{2}\right)(\widetilde{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))^{2}+\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))\,.

(4.43)

We now restrict the attention to
\Gamma_{1}
. By (
4.30
),
we have

\begin{split}&\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)=\mathrm{i}(\lambda t^{2}+\mu s^{2})+2\rho\,ts\,,\\
&\hbox{ for}\,\,(\xi,\eta)=((1+\mathrm{i})t,(1-\mathrm{i})s)=(\sqrt{2}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t,\sqrt{2}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}s)\in\Gamma_{1}\,.\end{split}

By (
4.32
) we have

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\asymp|\omega|^{2}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2}\hbox{ on }\Gamma_{1}\,.

(4.44)

The parametrization (
3.20
) becomes

\Gamma_{1}:\ \begin{cases}\omega=(1+\mathrm{i})t+(1+\mathrm{i})s=\sqrt{2}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}(t+s)\\
\widetilde{\omega}=(1+\mathrm{i})t-(1+\mathrm{i})s=\sqrt{2}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}(t-s)\,,\end{cases}\quad t,s\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(4.45)

so
\Gamma_{1}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\times\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}_{\widetilde{\omega}}
is a Cartesian product. From the fact
that
\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})
is a quadratic polynomial in
\widetilde{\omega}
for
every fixed
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
, with
\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\geq|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)
, when

\widetilde{\omega}\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
,

|\widetilde{\omega}|\gg|\omega|
and “the fundamental lemma”
(see
[
MeSj75_01
, Lemma 2.1, p. 145]
), we deduce that

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))\geq\inf_{\widetilde{\omega}\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\,,

(4.46)

for every
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
. In particular, by (
4.44
),

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega))\asymp|\omega|^{2},\quad\omega\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\,.

(4.47)

We now turn to the holomorphic function
\phi
, defined in

\mathrm{neigh}\big((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\omega,\tilde{\omega}}\big)
(or
in
\mathrm{neigh}\big((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\xi,\eta}\big)
, depending on the choice of coordinates). The function
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
has a non-degenerate critical point
\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega)
for
\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
such that

\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega)=\widetilde{\omega}_{c}^{0}(\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(\omega^{2})\,,

(4.48)

so
\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\cdot)
is a holomorphic function with
differential
\widetilde{\omega}^{0}_{c}
at
\omega=0
. As in the
quadratic case, we have

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega))\geq\inf_{\widetilde{\omega}\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\cap\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})}\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\,,

(4.49)

for
\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
,

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega))\asymp|\omega|^{2},\ \ \omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R})\,.

(4.50)

Put

\psi(\omega)=\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega)),\ \ \psi_{2}(\omega)=\phi_{2}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}^{0}_{c}(\omega))

(4.51)

and notice that

\psi(\omega)=\psi_{2}(\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(\omega^{3})\,.

(4.52)

We now study
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)
in (
4.21
). To start with, we
assume only that
a
is smooth and
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
in

\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\Gamma_{1})
and that
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}

has its support in a small neighborhood of
(0,0)
. (Similarly we could here weaken the assumption on
\phi
.) The infimum in (
4.49
) is attained at a point
\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\omega)\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\cap\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
and we have

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})-\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\omega))\asymp|\widetilde{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathrm{inf}}|^{2},\ \ \widetilde{\omega}\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\cap\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})\,.

(4.53)

It follows that

\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}}(a\chi)(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}={\mathcal{O}}(h^{\frac{1}{2}})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{h}\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\omega))}\,,

(4.54)

for
\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
. Observe here that

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathrm{inf}(\omega)})\asymp|\omega|^{2},\quad\omega\in\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\cap\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})\,.

(4.55)

We now strengthen the assumption on
a
and assume that

a(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})={\mathcal{O}}(1)\hbox{ is holomorphic in }\mathrm{neigh}\big((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2}\big)\,.

(4.56)

We allow
a
to depend on
h
and then assume that
a(\omega,\widetilde{\omega};h)
is a holomorphic classical symbol of order

h^{0}
.

We assume that
\chi
is supported in the neighborhood in
(
4.56
) and that

\chi=1\hbox{ in }\mathrm{neigh}\big((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\omega,\tilde{\omega}}\big)\,.

(4.57)

The method of steepest descent (and analytic stationary phase, see
[
19
, Théorème 2.8, p. 16]
) gives

\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}}(a\chi)(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\,b(\omega;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega))}\,,

(4.58)

for
\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
where
b(\omega;h)
is a holomorphic
classical symbol of order
0
,

b(\omega;h)\sim b_{0}(\omega)+hb_{1}(\omega)+...\,,

(4.59)

where

b_{0}(\omega)=\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}}{\sqrt{\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}^{2}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega))}}\,a_{0}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega))\,.

(4.60)

(By (
4.43
) we know that
\mathrm{Re}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}^{2}\phi)>0
and we choose the branch of the square root with the
same property.)
Here
a_{0}
is equal to
a
when
a
is independent of
h
and more
generally equal to the leading term in the asymptotics expansion
of
a
as a holomorphic classical symbol. Further,

\partial_{\overline{\omega}}b={\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{Ch}}),\hbox{ where }C>0.

(4.61)

Since
\Gamma_{1}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
, we
can apply Fubini’s theorem to the integral (
4.21
) to get

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)&=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{1}{\omega-\zeta}\,J(\omega)\,\mathrm{d}\omega\,,\hbox{ where }\\
J(\omega)&=\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}}(a\chi)(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\end{split}

(4.62)

and then apply (
4.54
), (
4.55
), (
4.58
) to the
inner integral.

We sum up the discussion about
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)
in
(
4.21
), (
4.22
).

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume that
\phi
is holomorphic in
\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);{\mathbb{C}}^{2})
with a critical point at
(0,0)
and Taylor expansion as in (
4.23
), (
4.24
), (
4.25
), (
4.26
) and (
4.27
). Then

\mathrm{Im}\,\phi(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\asymp|\omega|^{2}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2}\hbox{ on }\mathrm{neigh}\big((0,0);\Gamma_{1}\big)

(4.63)

and
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
has a non-degenerate critical point

\widetilde{\omega}_{c}(\omega)
near
0
for
\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
which satisfies (
4.50
).

Let
a(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})
be holomorphic in

\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2})
, either independent of
h
or a
holomorphic classical symbol of order
h^{0}
,

a(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})\sim a_{0}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})+ha_{1}(\omega,\widetilde{\omega})+...\,\,.

In view of (
4.50
)
\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\psi(\omega)}
behaves
like a Gaussian along
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
with its
peak at
\omega=0
. Recall that
|\zeta|
is small.

Let
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
be supported in a sufficiently
small neighborhood of
(0,0)
, where the assumptions on
a
and
\phi
are valid. Assume that
\chi=1
in another small neighborhood of

(0,0)
. Then
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)
is given by an iterated integral
as in (
4.62
), where the inner integral
J
satisfies

\partial_{\overline{\omega}}J={\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{{\mathcal{O}}(h)}}),\quad\omega\in\mathrm{neigh}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C})\,.

(4.64)

Moreover there is a neighborhood
\Omega
of
0
in
{\mathbb{C}}
, where we
have (
4.58
), (
4.59
), (
4.60
), (
4.61
). If

V\Subset\Omega
is another neighborhood of
0
, then

J={\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{{\mathcal{O}}(h)}})\hbox{ in }\,\,\mathrm{neigh}\big((\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R})\setminus V;\mathbb{C}\big)\,.

We next look at the asymptotics of
\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)
in
(
4.62
). Inserting (
4.58
) there gives

\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\omega-\zeta}h^{\frac{1}{2}}b(\omega;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\psi(\omega)}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\,,

(4.65)

where
b
satisfies (
4.59
)–(
4.61
) and we have defined
\psi
in (
4.51
).

For
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
small and outside a conic neighborhood of

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)|\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\zeta|+h^{1/2}+t}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{h}}\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

where we also used that we can deform
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
in a

h^{1/2}
neighborhood of
0
in a such a way that
|\zeta-\omega|\geq\frac{h^{1/2}}{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
along the deformed contour. Put

t=\sqrt{h}\,s
:

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)|={\mathcal{O}}(h^{\frac{1}{2}})\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda+s}\,\mathrm{e}^{-s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s,\,\hbox{ where }\,\lambda:=\frac{|\zeta|}{\sqrt{h}}+1\geq 1\,.

Here the integral is

\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda}\,\mathrm{e}^{-s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s={\mathcal{O}}(1)/\lambda

and also

\geq\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\lambda+s}\,\mathrm{e}^{-s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\geq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{O}}(\lambda)}\,.

We retain that

|\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)|=\frac{h^{1/2}{\mathcal{O}}(1)}{\lambda}=\frac{{\mathcal{O}}(h)}{|\zeta|+h^{1/2}}\,.

(4.66)

Let
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(D(0,1))
be
=1
in
D(0,1/2)
. Let

0<\delta\ll 1
be a constant. We shall study

\mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{new}}(\zeta)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\omega-\zeta}\,b(\omega;h)\chi(h^{\delta-1/2}\omega)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\psi(\omega)}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\,,

(4.67)

where we restrict
\zeta
to
D(0,h^{-\delta+1/2}/4)
from now on.
Observe that

\mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{new}}(\zeta)-\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)={\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}),\hbox{ for }\zeta\in D\Big(0,\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}}{4}\Big)\,.

We make the dilation
\omega=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega}
and
define
\widehat{\zeta}
by
\zeta=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\zeta}
. Put

\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega})=\frac{1}{h}\psi(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega})\,.

(4.68)

We have the Taylor series expansion, with
\psi_{2}=\varphi_{2}
,

\psi(\omega)=\psi_{2}(\omega)+r(\omega)\sim\psi_{2}(\omega)+\psi_{3}(\omega)+\psi_{4}(\omega)+\ldots\,,

(4.69)

where
\psi_{k}(\omega)=\dfrac{\psi^{(k)}(0)}{k!}\omega^{k}
is homogeneous of
degree
k
and
r(\omega)={\mathcal{O}}(\omega^{3})
.
It follows that

\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega};h)=\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})+h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega};h),\quad h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega};h)=\frac{1}{h}r(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega})

(4.70)

for
\widehat{\omega}={\mathcal{O}}(h^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, where

h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega};h)=\sum_{j=3}^{+\infty}h^{-1+\frac{j}{2}}\psi_{j}(\widehat{\omega})\,.

(4.71)

Since
\psi_{j}
is homogeneous of degree
j
, this can be viewed as an
asymptotic formula in
D(0,h^{-\delta})
. More precisely, put

h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})=\sum_{j=3}^{N-1}h^{-1+\frac{j}{2}}\psi_{j}(\widehat{\omega})\,.

Then

h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega})-h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})={\mathcal{O}}\big(h^{-1+N(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)}\big)\hbox{ in
}D(0,h^{-\delta})\,.

(4.72)

In particular,

h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega}),\ h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{N}(\widehat{\omega})={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{3(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1}={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{\frac{1}{2}-3\delta}=o(1)\,,

(4.73)

where from now on we assume that
\delta<1/6
.

Put
\exp^{(N)}(t)=\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}
. When
t={\mathcal{O}}(1)

we have

\exp^{(N)}(t)-\exp t={\mathcal{O}}(1)t^{N}\,.

We estimate

\displaystyle\exp^{(N)}\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\big)-\exp\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega})\big)=

\displaystyle\left(\exp^{(N)}\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\big)-\exp\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\big)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\exp\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\big)-\exp\big(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega})\big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:A+B\,.

Here for
\widehat{\omega}\in D(0,h^{-\delta})
,

A={\mathcal{O}}(1)\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\right)^{N}={\mathcal{O}}(1)\left(h^{(\frac{1}{2}-3\delta)}\right)^{N}={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{(\frac{1}{2}-3\delta)N}\,,

B={\mathcal{O}}(1)\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})-h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega})\right)={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{N(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1}\,,

hence

\exp^{(N)}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega}))-\exp(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega}))={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{(\frac{1}{2}-3\delta)N-1}\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta})\,.

(4.74)

We next expand

\displaystyle\exp^{(N)}\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{k!}\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\right)^{k}=\sum_{0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\sum_{j=3}^{N-1}\frac{1}{h}\psi_{j}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega})\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\quad 3\leq j_{1},...,j_{k}\leq N-1}\widetilde{c}_{k,j_{1},..,j_{k}}\left(\frac{1}{h}\psi_{j_{1}}(\widehat{\omega})\right)...\left(\frac{1}{h}\psi_{j_{k}}(\widehat{\omega})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\sum_{\quad 3\leq j_{1},...,j_{k}\leq N-1}c_{k,j_{1},..,j_{k}}h^{-k+\frac{j_{1}}{2}+...+\frac{j_{k}}{2}}\widehat{\omega}^{j_{1}+...+j_{k}}\,.

With
l=j_{1}+...+j_{k}\geq 3k
, we get with new coefficients

c_{k,l}
:

\exp^{(N)}\left(h^{1/2}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega}))\right)=\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{l=3k}^{3(N-1)}c_{k,l}h^{-k+l/2}\widehat{\omega}^{l}\,.

(4.75)

With
-k+l/2=:j/2
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we get

\frac{l}{2}=\frac{j}{2}+k\leq\frac{j}{2}+\frac{l}{3}\Longleftrightarrow\frac{l}{6}\leq\frac{j}{2}\,,

then

l\leq 3j\,.

(4.76)

Hence,

\exp^{(N)}\left(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})\right)=\sum_{j\geq 0}h^{\frac{j}{2}}p_{j}(\widehat{\omega})\,,

(4.77)

where
p_{j}
is a polynomial of degree
\leq 3j
and the sum is
finite. The
p_{j}
also depend on
N
;
p_{j}=p_{j}^{(N)}
, but we can
apply (
4.74
) with
\delta=0
, to see that

p_{j}^{(N)}(\widehat{\omega})=p_{j}^{(M)}(\widehat{\omega}),\hbox{
when }M\geq N

(4.78)

and
h^{j/2}>h^{-1+N/2},
i.e. when
j<N-2
. In other words,

p_{j}^{N}=p_{j}
is independent of
N
when
N>j+2
.

It follows that

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{r}(\widehat{\omega};h)}\sim 1+h^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{1}(\widehat{\omega})+hp_{2}(\widehat{\omega})+\ldots\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta}),

(4.79)

where
p_{j}
is a polynomial of degree
\leq 3j
.

Similarly, define

\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)=b(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega};h)

(4.80)

then
\mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{new}}(\zeta;h)
becomes

\widehat{\mathrm{I}}(\widehat{\zeta};h)=-\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\,\frac{\chi(h^{\delta}\widehat{\omega})}{\widehat{\omega}-\widehat{\zeta}}\,\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega};h)}\mathrm{d}\widehat{\omega}\,.

(4.81)

From the Taylor expansion of
b
in (
4.80
), we get

\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)\sim\widehat{b}_{0}(\widehat{\omega};h)+h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{b}_{1}(\widehat{\omega};h)+...\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta})\,,

where
b_{j}
is a polynomial of degree
\leq j
,
hence using also (
4.79
)

\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega};h)}=d(\widehat{\omega};h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\,,

(4.82)

where

d(\widehat{\omega};h)\sim d_{0}(\widehat{\omega})+h^{\frac{1}{2}}d_{1}(\widehat{\omega})+\ldots\,\hbox{ in }\,D(0,h^{-\delta})\,,

and
d_{j}(\widehat{\omega})
is a computable polynomial of degree

\leq 3j
.

Recall that
\psi_{2}(\omega)
is given by (
4.51
), (
4.39
), hence

\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})=f\dfrac{\widehat{\omega}^{2}}{2}
,

f=\frac{\lambda\mu+\rho^{2}}{\lambda+\mu+2i\rho}\,.

(4.83)

We have

\widehat{\omega}=\frac{1}{f}\partial_{\widehat{\omega}}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})\,,

(4.84)

and asymptoticsally,

d(\widehat{\omega};h)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}=c(f^{-1}D_{\hat{\omega}};h)\left(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\right),

(4.85)

where
c
has the same properties and the same leading term as
d
,

c(\widehat{\omega};h)\sim c_{0}(\widehat{\omega})+h^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{1}(\widehat{\omega})+\ldots\,\hbox{ in }\,D(0,h^{-\delta})\,,

(4.86)

and
c_{j}(\widehat{\omega})
is a computable polynomial of degree

\leq 3j
.

In fact,

\widehat{\omega}^{k}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}=(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\omega}})^{k}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}+g_{k}(\widehat{\omega})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\,,

where
g_{k}
is a polynomial of degree
\leq k-1
and by recursion, we
find a polynomial
c_{k}
of the same degree as
d_{k}
such that

c_{k}(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\omega}})(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})})=d_{k}(\widehat{\omega})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\,.

Now use (
4.81
), (
4.82
), (
4.85
), to get

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathrm{I}}(\widehat{\zeta};h)&=-\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{\chi(h^{\delta}\widehat{\omega})}{\widehat{\omega}-\widehat{\zeta}}\,d(\widehat{\omega};h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\omega}\\
&=-\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{\chi(h^{\delta}\widehat{\omega})}{\widehat{\omega}-\widehat{\zeta}}\,c(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\omega}};h)\left(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\omega}\\
&=h^{\frac{1}{2}}c(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\zeta}};h)\left(G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})\right)+{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\infty}),\,\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)\,,\end{split}

(4.87)

where

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{1}{\widehat{\zeta}-\widehat{\omega}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\omega},\,\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)\,.

(4.88)

The
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\infty})
remainder comes from the cutoff and from the
asymptotics nature of
c
. We also used (when necessary) that the
contour
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
can be deformed in a fixed neighborhood
of
0
.
Here we recall that

\widehat{\zeta}\neq 0\hbox{ and }\widehat{\zeta}\,\hbox{ is not in a conic neighborhood of }\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\Big({\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}\Big)\,.

(4.89)

When
\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}
is replaced by
-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}
and

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
by
{\mathbb{R}}
, we have, modulo the multiplicative factor
2\pi\mathrm{i}
, the special functions
G_{l/r}
discussed in
[
13
]

and as there we are in the presence of two special functions

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{l/r}
where
G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{l}
is
defined as in (
4.88
) after possibly deforming the contour

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
slightly so that the deformed contour avoids
\widehat{\zeta}
and passes with this point to the left when oriented in the direction of increasing
\mathrm{Re}\,\widehat{\omega}
. Likewise

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{r}
is defined, with the deformed contour
passing with
\widehat{\zeta}
to the right. See Appendix
B
.

When the contour is equal to
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
and

\widehat{\zeta}
satisfies (
4.89
), we get

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{l}(\widehat{\zeta})
when
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta})>0
and

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{r}(\widehat{\zeta})
when
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta})<0
.

By the residue theorem we have for
\widehat{\zeta}\in D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)
,

G^{r}_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})-G^{l}_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})=\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\zeta})}\,.

(4.90)

Similarly we can define
\widehat{I}^{l,r}(\widehat{\zeta};h)

directly from (
4.81
) and then

\widehat{\mathrm{I}}^{r}(\widehat{\zeta})-\widehat{\mathrm{I}}^{l}(\widehat{\zeta})=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\widehat{b}(\widehat{\zeta};h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\zeta};h)}\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)\,.

(4.91)

From (
4.87
), (
4.88
), we get
under the assumption (
4.89
)

\widehat{\mathrm{I}}(\widehat{\zeta};h)=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\,c(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\zeta}};h)\Big(G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})\Big)+\mathcal{O}(h^{\infty})\hbox{
in }D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)\,,

(4.92)

choosing the branch “
l
” for
\widehat{\mathrm{I}}
and
G
when

\mathrm{Im}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta}\big)>0
and the branch “
r
” when

\mathrm{Im}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta}\big)<0
.

We rewrite this expansion in terms of the Dawson-type functions

G^{l}
,
G^{r}
(more precisely these are the Hilbert transforms of the
Dawson integral, see e.g.,
[
1
]
) and start with a general remark (dropping the hats): Let

\rho(\omega)=r\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\,,\quad 0\neq r\in{\mathbb{C}}\,.

(4.93)

Then
\{\omega\in{\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}};\,\mathrm{Re}\rho(\omega)<0\}
is the
disjoint union of two sectors
R_{-}
and
R_{+}
. Put

G^{l/r}_{\rho}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\gamma}\frac{1}{\zeta-\omega}\mathrm{e}^{\rho(\omega)}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\,,

(4.94)

where
\gamma=a+b{\mathbb{R}}
,
b\neq 0
is a straight line, oriented by
the natural orientation of
{\mathbb{R}}
, such that for
\gamma(t)=a+bt
,

\gamma(t)\in R_{\pm}\hbox{ and }\mathrm{dist}(\gamma(t),{\mathbb{C}}\setminus R_{\pm})\asymp|t|,\hbox{ when }\pm t\gg 1\,,

(4.95)

\begin{split}&\gamma(t)\hbox{ avoids }\zeta\hbox{ and passes with that point to
the}\\
&\hbox{ left/right, when }t\hbox{ increases from }-\infty\hbox{ to
}+\infty.\end{split}

(4.96)

The value of
G_{\rho}^{l/r}(\zeta)
only depends on the homotopy
class of
\gamma
with these properties, so
G_{\rho}^{l/r}
are
entire functions related via the the Cauchy formula,

G_{\rho}^{l}(\zeta)-G_{\rho}^{r}(\zeta)=-\mathrm{e}^{\rho(\zeta)}\,.

(4.97)

These definitions extend those of
G^{l/r}=G^{l/r}_{-\zeta^{2}/2}
,
G^{l/r}_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}
above, if we adopt the natural
orientations of
{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.

Let

\widetilde{\rho}(\widetilde{\omega})=\widetilde{r}\,\frac{\widetilde{\omega}^{2}}{2}\,,\quad 0\neq\widetilde{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}\,.

(4.98)

Then
\{\widetilde{\omega}\in\dot{{\mathbb{C}}};\,\mathrm{Re}\widetilde{\rho}(\widetilde{\omega})<0\}
is the disjoint union of two sectors

\widetilde{R}_{-}
and
\widetilde{R}_{+}
. Define
G^{l/r}_{\widetilde{\rho}}
as in (
4.94
). We have

\widetilde{\rho}(\widetilde{\omega})=\rho(\omega)\hbox{ when
}\widetilde{\omega}=(r/\widetilde{r})^{1/2}\omega,

(4.99)

and we choose the branch of the square root for which

\widetilde{R}_{\pm}=(r/\widetilde{r})^{1/2}R_{\pm}
. If
\widetilde{\zeta}=(r/\widetilde{r})^{1/2}\zeta
, we get

G^{l/r}_{\widetilde{\rho}}(\widetilde{\zeta})=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\zeta}-\widetilde{\omega}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}(\widetilde{\omega})}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\gamma}\frac{1}{\zeta-\omega}\,\mathrm{e}^{\rho(\omega)}\,\mathrm{d}\omega=G_{\rho}(\zeta)\,.

(4.100)

We apply this to the case
\widehat{\rho}(\widehat{\omega})=\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})
(replacing tildes by hats)
\rho(\omega)=\penalty\ -\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}
. We have
\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})=\mathrm{i}f\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{2}}{2}=:\widehat{r}\,\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{2}}{2}

with
f
as in (
4.83
),
\rho(\omega)=r\,\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}
,
r=-1
. The
change of variables in (
4.99
) is

\widehat{\omega}=(r/\widehat{r})^{1/2}\omega=(\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\omega\,.

(4.101)

We have
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{i}/f)>0
and choose the branch of
(\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}
in
the first quadrant,
0<\arg\big((\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\big)<\pi/2
. Then
(
4.100
) gives

G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}^{l/r}(\widehat{\zeta})=G_{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}^{l/r}(\zeta),\hbox{ when }\,\widehat{\zeta}=(\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\zeta\,.

(4.102)

We now make the same change of variable in (
4.85
). From (
4.101
) we get

D_{\widehat{\omega}}=(f/\mathrm{i})^{1/2}D_{\omega}=\mathrm{i}(f/\mathrm{i})^{1/2}\partial_{\omega}=(\mathrm{i}f)^{1/2}\partial_{\omega}\,,

(4.103)

and (
4.85
) gives

d(\widehat{\omega};h)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}=c\big((\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\partial_{\omega};h\big)(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}})\,.

(4.104)

Substitution in (
4.87
) gives

\hskip-15.0pt\widehat{\mathrm{I}}^{l/r}(\widehat{\zeta};h)=h^{1/2}c\big((\frac{\mathrm{i}}{f})^{1/2}\partial_{\zeta^{\dagger}};h\big)\Big(G_{-\frac{(\zeta^{\dagger})^{2}}{2}}^{l/r}(\zeta^{\dagger})\Big)+{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\infty}),\hbox{ in
}D_{\widehat{\zeta}}(0,\frac{h^{-\delta}}{4})\,,

(4.105)

where

\widehat{\zeta}=(\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\zeta^{\dagger}\,.

(4.106)

Proposition 4.4
.

Recall the expression (
4.65
) for

\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)
,
where
b
,
\psi
fulfill (
4.51
), (
4.59
)–(
4.61
).
Let
\psi_{2}(\omega)=f\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}
be the leading
term in the Taylor expansion (
4.69
) of
\psi
. Here
f
is
given in (
4.83
).

We make the dilation
\omega=h^{1/2}\widehat{\omega}
and
define
\widehat{\zeta}
by
\zeta=h^{1/2}\widehat{\zeta}
. Define

\widehat{\psi}
as in (
4.68
). Let
0\leq\delta<1/6
. Then

\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega})=\frac{1}{h}\psi(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\omega})=\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})+{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\frac{1}{2}-3\delta})\hbox{ in }D(0,h^{-\delta})\,.

Put
\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)=b(h^{1/2}\widehat{\omega};h)

and define
\widehat{\mathrm{I}}(\widehat{\zeta};h)
as in (
4.81
). Then

\widehat{\mathrm{I}}(\widehat{\zeta};h)-\mathrm{I}(\zeta;h)={\mathcal{O}}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\big),\quad\zeta\in D(0,\frac{1}{4}h^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta})\,.

(4.107)

We have asymptoticsally in
D(0,h^{-\delta})
,

\widehat{b}(\widehat{\omega};h)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\widehat{\psi}(\widehat{\omega};h)}=c(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\omega}};h)\left(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}(\widehat{\omega})}\right)\,,

(4.108)

where
\widehat{c}(\widehat{\omega};h)\sim c_{0}(\widehat{\omega})+h^{1/2}c_{1}(\widehat{\omega})+...\,
in
D(0,h^{-\delta})
and
c_{j}(\widehat{\omega})
is a polynomial of degree

\leq 3j
(as in
(
4.86
)).

Uniformly for
\widehat{\zeta}\in D(0,h^{-\delta}/4)

satisfying (
4.89
),

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}(\zeta,1)&=h^{\frac{1}{2}}c(f^{-1}D_{\widehat{\zeta}};h)\left(G_{\mathrm{i}\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\zeta})\right)+{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\infty})\\
&=h^{1/2}c((\mathrm{i}/f)^{1/2}\partial_{\zeta^{\dagger}};h)\Big(G^{l/r}_{-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}}(\zeta^{\dagger})\Big)+{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\infty})\,,\end{split}

(4.109)

where

\widehat{\zeta}=(i/f)^{1/2}\zeta^{\dagger}
.
The functions
G_{\rho}=G_{\rho}^{l/r}
are defined by line integrals in
(
4.88
), (
4.94
), and we take
G_{..}=G_{..}^{l}
when

\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta})>0
and

G_{..}=G_{..}^{r}
when
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\widehat{\zeta})<0
.

5
The term
\mathrm{II}(\zeta,1-\delta)
in (
3
)

In this section we discuss the second term in the
decomposition of section
3
for various values of the
parameter
\varepsilon:=|\zeta|/\sqrt{h}
.

As in Proposition
3.5
, let
a
,
\phi
be holomorphic on some
neighborhood of
(0,0)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\omega,\tilde{\omega}}
and
let
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\omega,\tilde{\omega}})

have its support in that neighborhood. More assumptions will be made below.

Recall that in (
3
),

\mathrm{II}(\zeta,1-\delta)=-\int_{0}^{1-\delta}\hskip-5.0pta(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta};h)\chi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})}\,\dfrac{2\mathrm{i}\,\overline{\widetilde{\zeta}}}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(5.1)

where
(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})\in\Gamma_{\theta}
so
\widetilde{\zeta}
is given by
(
3.43
):

\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta)=\frac{2\mathrm{i}\theta}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\zeta+\frac{1+\theta^{2}}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\overline{\zeta}\,.

Let us make a first change of variable:

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\alpha}:=\frac{1+\mathrm{i}\theta}{1-\mathrm{i}\theta}=\frac{1-\theta^{2}}{1+\theta^{2}}+\mathrm{i}\frac{2\theta}{1+\theta^{2}}\,,\qquad\mathrm{i.e.}\quad\begin{cases}\cos\alpha=\dfrac{1-\theta^{2}}{1+\theta^{2}}\\
\sin\alpha=\dfrac{2\theta}{1+\theta^{2}}\\
\end{cases}

with
\theta\in[0,1]
and
\alpha\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
.
Since
\theta=\tan(\frac{\alpha}{2})
,

\mathrm{d}\theta=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\alpha}{(\cos\frac{\alpha}{2})^{2}}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\alpha}{1+\cos\alpha}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\alpha}{1+\frac{1-\theta^{2}}{1+\theta^{2}}}\,,

(1+\theta^{2})^{-1}\mathrm{d}\theta=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\alpha}{2}

and we get

\frac{2}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta=\frac{1}{\cos\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\alpha\,.

(5.2)

Then (
3.43
) becomes with some abuse of notation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta)=\widetilde{\zeta}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\frac{\sin\alpha}{\cos\alpha}\,\,\zeta+\frac{1}{\cos\alpha}\,\overline{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\tan\alpha\,\zeta+\sqrt{1+(\tan\alpha)^{2}}\,\,\overline{\zeta}\,.

(5.3)

We introduce the change of variable:

\begin{cases}t=\tan\alpha\\
\mathrm{d}t=(1+(\tan\alpha)^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha\\
\alpha\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}[\end{cases}\iff\quad\begin{cases}\alpha=\arctan(t)\\
\mathrm{d}\alpha=\dfrac{1}{1+t^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
t\in[0,+\infty[\end{cases}\,.

(5.4)

(
5.2
) gives

\frac{2}{1-\theta^{2}}\mathrm{d}\theta=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}\mathrm{d}t\,.

(5.5)

(
3.43
), (
5
) become with some abuse of notation

\begin{split}\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta)=\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,\theta)=\widetilde{\zeta}(t)=\widetilde{\zeta}(t,\zeta)&=\mathrm{i}t\,\zeta+\sqrt{1+t^{2}}\,\,\overline{\zeta}\\
&=\mathrm{i}t\,\zeta+t\,\overline{\zeta}+\big(\sqrt{1+t^{2}}-t\big)\,\overline{\zeta}\\
&=t(\mathrm{i}\zeta+\overline{\zeta})+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}+t}\,\overline{\zeta}\,.\end{split}

(5.6)

With
0\not=\zeta=|\zeta|\sigma
,
\sigma\in S^{1}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\widetilde{\zeta}(t)&=|\zeta|\left((\mathrm{i}\sigma+\overline{\sigma})\,t+\overline{\sigma}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}+t}\right)\\
&=|\zeta|\left(2s_{+}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\right)\,,\\
\end{split}

(5.7)

where

\sigma=s_{+}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}+s_{-}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\hbox{ with }s_{\pm}=\mathrm{Re}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\sigma\right),\hbox{ satisfying}\,\,\,\,s_{+}^{2}+s_{-}^{2}=1\,,

(5.8)

and

g_{1}(t):=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}+t}\,,\ \ t\geq 0\,,

(5.9)

satisfies

\partial_{t}^{k}g_{1}(t)={\mathcal{O}}\left((1+t)^{-1-k}\right),

(5.10)

for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Remark 5.1
.

We can change the variable directly between
\theta\in[0,1[
and
t\in[0,+\infty[
by:

\begin{cases}t=\dfrac{2\theta}{1-\theta^{2}},\\
\mathrm{d}t=2\dfrac{1+\theta^{2}}{(1-\theta^{2})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta=\dfrac{1+\theta^{2}}{1-\theta^{2}}\dfrac{2}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.\end{cases}

(5.11)

Since

1+t^{2}=1+\dfrac{4\theta^{2}}{(1-\theta^{2})^{2}}=\dfrac{(1-\theta^{2})^{2}+4\theta^{2}}{(1-\theta^{2})^{2}}=\left(\dfrac{1+\theta^{2}}{1-\theta^{2}}\right)^{2}\,,

we get (
5.5
).

We obtain the following result:

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
. Then

s_{+}\neq 0
, so
\widetilde{\zeta}(t)\to\infty
when
t\to+\infty

and
\mathrm{II}(\zeta,1)
is well defined and equal to

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}a(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta);h)\,&\chi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta))\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta))}\,\dfrac{2\mathrm{i}\,\overline{\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta)}}{1-\theta^{2}}d\theta\\
&=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\hskip-5.0pta(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(t);h)\,\chi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(t))\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\dfrac{\mathrm{i}\overline{\widetilde{\zeta}(t)}}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}t\,,\end{split}

(5.12)

where

\varphi(\zeta,t)=\mathrm{i}\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))

(5.13)

and

\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)
is given by (
5.7
), (
5.8
) and (
5.9
).

We now make the more precise assumptions (
4.23
),
(
4.24
), (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.27
),
(
4.56
), (
4.57
) and use (
4.33
), (
4.34
).

Recall that
\xi=\frac{\zeta+\widetilde{\zeta}}{2}
,

\eta=\frac{\zeta-\widetilde{\zeta}}{2\mathrm{i}}
and that the quadratic part of the phase
\phi
is given by (
4.26
), (
4.34
):

\begin{split}\phi_{2}(\xi,\eta)&=\frac{\lambda}{2}\xi^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}\eta^{2}+\rho\xi\eta\\
&=\frac{\lambda}{8}(\zeta+\widetilde{\zeta})^{2}+\frac{\mu}{8}(\zeta-\widetilde{\zeta})^{2}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{4}(\zeta+\widetilde{\zeta})(\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta)\,,\end{split}

(5.14)

where
\lambda,\,\mu>0
,
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
.
Rewrite
\zeta+\widetilde{\zeta}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta
, using (
5.7
):

\zeta+\widetilde{\zeta}=|\zeta|\,\Big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)+\sigma\Big)\,,

(5.15)

\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta=|\zeta|\Big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)-\sigma\Big)\,.

(5.16)

Then,

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t):=\mathrm{i}

\displaystyle\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}

\displaystyle|\zeta|^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda}{8}\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)+\sigma\right)^{2}+\frac{\mu}{8}\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)-\sigma\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{4}\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)+\sigma\right)\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)-\sigma\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}

\displaystyle|\zeta|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{\lambda+\mu}{8}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{4}\right)\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\right)^{2}+\frac{\lambda-\mu}{4}\sigma\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{\lambda+\mu}{8}-\mathrm{i}\frac{\rho}{4}\right)\sigma^{2}\right).

(5.17)

We complete the square from the first two terms inside the large parenthesis
in the last expression and get after some computations,

\begin{split}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=&|\zeta|^{2}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\\
=&\mathrm{i}\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{8}\left((\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)\left(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)+\frac{(\lambda-\mu)\sigma}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\right)^{2}\right.\\
&\left.+(\lambda+\mu-\mathrm{i}2\rho)\left(1-\frac{(\lambda-\mu)^{2}}{(\lambda+\mu)^{2}+4\rho^{2}}\right)\sigma^{2}\right)\,,\end{split}

(5.18)

\zeta=|\zeta|\sigma
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\zeta=|\zeta|\,\sigma\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}=

\displaystyle\exp\left({\frac{\mathrm{i}(\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)}{8}\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\left((2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}})\,t+\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\sigma\right)^{2}}\right)

(5.19)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left({\frac{\mathrm{i}(\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)}{8}\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\big(\upsilon_{0}(\sigma,t)+\upsilon_{1}(t)+\upsilon_{2}(\sigma,t)\big)}\right),

where
\upsilon_{0},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2}
are smooth bounded functions, given by:

\displaystyle\upsilon_{0}(\sigma,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\lambda\mu+\rho^{2}}{(\lambda+\mu)^{2}+4\rho^{2}}\sigma^{2}+4s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)t=\mathcal{O}(1)

(5.20)

\displaystyle\upsilon_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=2\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}g_{1}(t)\underset{+\infty}{\sim}\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}.\frac{1}{t}

(5.21)

\displaystyle\upsilon_{2}(\sigma,t)

\displaystyle=\overline{\sigma}^{2}g_{1}(t)^{2}\underset{+\infty}{\sim}\frac{\overline{\sigma}^{2}}{4t^{2}}\,,

(5.22)

satisfying

\partial^{k}\upsilon_{\nu}={\mathcal{O}}((1+t)^{-\nu-k})\hbox{ on }[0,+\infty[\hbox{ for }\ \nu=0,1,2,\ k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(5.23)

The functions
\upsilon_{\nu}
belong to the symbol space
\mathbb{S}^{-\nu}=\mathbb{S}^{-\nu}([0,+\infty[)
in the sense of the following definition,

Definition 5.4
.

For
T_{0}\in[0,+\infty[
, we put

\mathbb{S}^{m}=\mathbb{S}^{m}([T_{0},+\infty[)=(1+t)^{m}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T_{0},+\infty[)\\
=\{u\in C^{\infty}([T_{0},+\infty[);\,\partial_{t}^{k}u=\mathcal{O}((1+t)^{m-k}),\,\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\}\,.

(5.24)

Here
m\in\mathbb{R}
. If
F(t)>0
,
T_{0}\leq t<\infty,
we put
F\mathbb{S}^{m}=\{Fu;\,u\in\mathbb{S}^{m}\}
. Clearly

(1+t)^{n}\mathbb{S}^{m}=\mathbb{S}^{m+n}

(5.25)

Summing up Proposition
5.2
and Lemma
5.3
, we obtain
the following
result when
\phi=\phi_{2}
:

Proposition 5.5
.

For
\zeta=|\zeta|\,\sigma\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
, we have

-\int_{0}^{1}a(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta);h)\,\chi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta))\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{h}\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta))}\,\dfrac{2\mathrm{i}\,\overline{\widetilde{\zeta}(\theta)}}{1-\theta^{2}}\,\,\mathrm{d}\theta\\
=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t)\exp\left(\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

(5.26)

where

b(\zeta,t)=a(\zeta,|\zeta|\big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big);h)\\
\times\chi\Big(\zeta,|\zeta|\big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big)\Big)|\zeta|\Big[2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\,t+\sigma g_{1}(t)\Big]\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}\,,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{(\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)}{8}\times

(5.28)

\displaystyle\Big(-\big(2s_{+}\,t+\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\sigma\big)^{2}+\mathrm{i}\big(\upsilon_{0}(\sigma,t)+\upsilon_{1}(t)+\upsilon_{2}(\sigma,t)\big)\Big)\,,

where
\upsilon_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{-j}([0,+\infty[\,\ j=0,1,2
are given by
(
5.20
)–(
5.22
) and
s_{+},g_{1}
are given by
(
5.8
) and (
5.9
) respectively.

5.1
Intermediate values of
\varepsilon

Set
\varepsilon:=\frac{|\zeta|}{\sqrt{h}}>0
. The case

\varepsilon\geq h^{-\delta}
for some small but fixed
\delta>0

has been treated in Section
2
, and we shall next study
the case when
\varepsilon\,
is of the order of 1, or more
precisely when

h^{\delta}\leq\varepsilon\,\leq h^{-\delta},

(5.29)

for some small fixed
\delta>0
. In this case we will not try to get the asymptotics of the integral in the left hand side of (
5.26
)
over the whole half line
[0,+\infty[
but rather for the integral
over a half line
[T,+\infty[
for a suitable not too large
T>0
and leave the integral over
[0,T]
for a numerical study.

We assume that
\zeta={\mathcal{O}}(1)
and
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus V
,
where
V
is a conical open neighborhood in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
of

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}{\mathbb{R}}
. Then

\mathrm{supp}\big[b(\zeta,\cdot)\big]\subset\Big[0,\frac{C}{|\zeta|}\Big],\quad\hbox{ for some }C>0.

(5.30)

We have

\partial_{t}^{k}(a\chi)(\zeta,|\zeta|\big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big);h)=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta|^{k})=\mathcal{O}(t^{-k}),\quad\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Since we also have
|\zeta|=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we get

\partial_{t}^{k}(a\chi)(\zeta,|\zeta|\big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big);h)=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta|^{k})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{(1+t)^{k}}),\,\,\,\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,,

i.e.,

(a\chi)(\zeta,|\zeta|\big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\cdot+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(\cdot)\big);h)\in\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[).

It follows that

\partial_{t}^{k}b(\zeta,t)=\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{|\zeta|}{(1+t)^{k}},\,\,\,\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,,

(5.31)

i.e.,
b\in|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[)
.

We next look at
\varphi_{2}
: We have
\upsilon_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[)
,
j=0,1,2
,

\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\equiv-\frac{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}{8}\Big(2s_{+}\,t+\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\sigma\Big)^{2}\ \mathrm{mod\,}\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[),

(5.32)

\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\equiv-\frac{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}{2}s_{+}\Big(2s_{+}\,t+\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\sigma\Big)\mathrm{mod\,}\mathbb{S}^{-1}([0,+\infty[)\,,

(5.33)

\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\in\mathbb{S}^{1}([0,+\infty[)\,.

(5.34)

The assumption that
\zeta
is not in a conical neighborhood of
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}]0,+\infty[
tells us that
|s_{+}|\geq\frac{1}{\mathcal{O}(1)}
. We have

-\partial_{t}\mathrm{Re}\,\varphi_{2}\asymp\big|\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}\big|\asymp t\qquad\mathrm{when}\quad t\geq T_{0}\gg 1\,,

(5.35)

and we get

\frac{1}{\mathrm{Re}\,(\partial_{t}\varphi_{2})}\,,\ \frac{1}{\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}\in\mathbb{S}^{-1}([T_{0},+\infty[)\,.

(5.36)

We can construct an asymptotics anti-derivative of

b\exp\big(\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}\big)
in the limit

t\rightarrow+\infty
. Look for a suitable
c(t,\zeta;h)
such that

\partial_{t}\Big(ce^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}}\Big)\approx be^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}}\,,

i.e.,

\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}(\partial_{t}\varphi_{2})c+\partial_{t}c\approx b\,,

c+\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}\partial_{t}c\approx\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}b\,.

As an approximate solution we take

c^{(N)}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\Big(-\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}\partial_{t}\Big)^{k}\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}b=:\sum_{k=0}^{N}c_{k}\,.

(5.37)

Then

c^{(N)}+\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}\partial_{t}c^{(N)}=\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}b+\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}\partial_{t}c_{N}\,.

(5.38)

We have on
[T_{0},+\infty[
,

\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}\in\mathbb{S}^{1},\quad(\partial_{t}\varphi_{2})^{-1}\in\mathbb{S}^{-1},\quad b\in|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}\,.

(5.39)

By (
5.39
), we get on
[T_{0},+\infty[
,

c_{k}\in\Big(\frac{h}{(t|\zeta|)^{2}}\Big)^{k}\frac{h}{t|\zeta|}\mathbb{S}^{0},

(5.40)

hence for
T\geq T_{0}

{{c_{k}}_{|}}_{[T,+\infty[}\in\Big(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\Big)^{k}\frac{h}{t|\zeta|}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T,+\infty[)\,.

(5.41)

Assume that
\displaystyle\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\leq 1
, i.e.,

T|\zeta|\geq\sqrt{h}
,
T\geq\frac{\sqrt{h}}{|\zeta|}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
.
If

T\geq\max\big(T_{0},\frac{\sqrt{h}}{|\zeta|}\big)\,,

(5.42)

we get

{{c_{k}}_{|}}_{[T,+\infty[}\in\frac{h}{t|\zeta|}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T,+\infty[),\hbox{ hence }{{c^{(N)}}_{|}}_{[T,+\infty[}\in\frac{h}{t|\zeta|}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T,+\infty[).

(5.43)

In particular,

c^{(0)}=c_{0}=\frac{h}{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}b\in\frac{h}{t|\zeta|}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T,+\infty[).

(5.44)

Returning to the original problem of integration, we write (
5.38
) as

\frac{|\zeta|^{2}\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}}{h}\,c^{(N)}+\partial_{t}c^{(N)}=b+\partial_{t}c_{N}\,,

or

\partial_{t}\Big(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}}c^{(N)}\Big)=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}}\big(b+\partial_{t}c_{N}\big)\,,

(5.45)

where by (
5.40
),

\partial_{t}c_{N}\in\Big(\frac{h}{(t|\zeta|)^{2}}\Big)^{N+1}\frac{|\zeta|}{t}\mathbb{S}^{0}([T,+\infty[)\,.

(5.46)

Recall that
b\in|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}
by (
5.39
).

Integrate (
5.45
) from
T
to
+\infty
:

\int_{T}^{+\infty}b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t=-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,T)}c^{(N)}(T)-\int_{T}^{+\infty}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\big(\partial_{t}c_{N}\big)(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

(5.47)

Assume first that
b
is an elliptic positive element of
|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}
:

0<b\asymp|\zeta|\,.

(5.48)

By (
5.46
) we get

\partial_{t}c_{N}=\mathcal{O}(1)\Big(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\Big)^{N+1}b

(5.49)

and (
5.47
), (
5.49
) give

\int_{T}^{+\infty}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\right)^{N+1}\right)b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t=-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,T)}c^{(N)}(T).

(5.50)

With
N=0
we get in view of (
5.43
),

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{\infty}\left(1+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\right)\right)b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,T)}c_{0}(T)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,T)}\frac{h}{T|\zeta|^{2}}|\zeta|\,.

(5.51)

In this discussion we can replace
\varphi_{2}
by
\mathrm{Re}(\varphi_{2})
, in particular in (
5.1
). Assume that
T\geq T_{0}
,

T\gg\frac{\sqrt{h}}{|\zeta|}
(cf. (
5.42
)). With
N=0
, we first get

\int_{T}^{+\infty}b(t)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}(\mathrm{Re}\,\varphi_{2})(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}(\mathrm{Re}\varphi_{2})(\sigma,t)}\frac{h}{T|\zeta|}\,,

which implies the estimate

\int_{T}^{+\infty}b(t)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\frac{h}{T|\zeta|}\,,

(5.52)

where we can weaken the ellipticity, positivity and symbol class
assumptions on
b
and merely assume that
b={\mathcal{O}}(|\zeta|)
on
[T,+\infty[
.

Using this and (
5.46
) to estimate the last integral in
(
5.47
), we get

Proposition 5.6
.

For
T\geq T_{0}
,
T\gg\sqrt{h}/|\zeta|
, we have for every
b\in|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
:

\int_{T}^{+\infty}b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t=-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,T)}\left(c^{(N)}(T)+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\right)^{N+1}\frac{h}{T|\zeta|}\right).

(5.53)

Here
c^{(N)}
is defined in (
5.37
) and we
recall (
5.43
).

We shall extend this result to the case when the quadratic phase

\phi_{2}
is replaced by the full phase
\phi
, where

\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})+r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})\,,

(5.54)

and

r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})={\mathcal{O}}((\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})^{3})

(5.55)

is holomorphic in
\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2})
and the cutoff has
its support in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
(0,0)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}_{\zeta,\tilde{\zeta}}
.

We study the symbol properties of
r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))
, where
\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)
is given by
(
5.7
) and
\zeta=|\zeta|\sigma
,

\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)=\left((i\zeta+\overline{\zeta})t+\overline{\zeta}g_{1}(t)\right),

(5.56)

g_{1}(t)=\left(t+\sqrt{1+t^{2}}\right)^{-1}\in\mathbb{S}^{-1}([0,+\infty[)
and
we keep the assumption that
|s_{+}(\zeta)|\geq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
, so that

|i\zeta+\overline{\zeta}|\asymp|\zeta|\,.

(5.57)

For any fixed
T>0
, we have

r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))={\mathcal{O}}((\zeta,\overline{\zeta})^{3}),\hbox{ uniformly for }t\in[0,T]\,,

(5.58)

and this function is real-analytic for
\zeta\in\mathrm{neigh}(0;\mathbb{C})
.

By (
5.56
),

\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{\zeta}^{k}\partial_{\overline{\zeta}}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{O}}(1)|\zeta|^{1-k-\ell}(1+t)^{1-j},\ \ 0\leq k+\ell\leq 1\\
0,\ \ k+\ell\geq 2\end{cases}

(5.59)

and this implies that

\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{\zeta}^{k}\partial_{\overline{\zeta}}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))={\mathcal{O}}(1)|\zeta|^{(1-k-\ell)_{+}}(1+t)^{1-j}\,.

(5.60)

For
T>0
sufficiently large, we have by (
5.56
), (
5.57
)

|\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)|\asymp|\zeta|t,\ \ t\in[T,+\infty[\,.

(5.61)

By (
5.55
) and Taylor expansion, we can write

r=\sum_{p+q=3}r_{p,q},

(5.62)

where
r_{p,q}
are holomorphic in a neighborhood of
(0,0)
and
satisfy

r_{p,q}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{p}\widetilde{\zeta}^{q},\ \ p,q\geq 0.

(5.63)

(If necessary we shrink the support of
\chi
, to be contained in a
neighborhood of
(0,0)
, where (
5.63
) holds.)

By the chain rule,

\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{\zeta}^{m}\partial_{\overline{\zeta}}^{\ell}&\left(r_{p,q}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\right)=\hbox{ a
finite linear combination of terms}\\
&\left(\partial_{\zeta}^{M}\partial_{\widetilde{\zeta}}^{L}\,r_{p,q}\right)(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\prod_{\nu=1}^{L}\left(\partial_{\zeta}^{m_{\nu}}\partial_{\overline{\zeta}}^{\ell_{\nu}}\partial_{t}^{j_{\nu}}\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)\right)\end{split}

(5.64)

with

m_{\nu}+\ell_{\nu}+j_{\nu}\neq 0,\ \ m_{\nu}+\ell_{\nu}\leq 1,

(5.65)

M+\sum_{\nu}m_{\nu}=m,\ \ \sum_{\nu}\ell_{\nu}=\ell,\ \ \sum_{\nu}j_{\nu}=j\,.

(5.66)

When
\ell=j=0
, we also have the simple term
\partial_{\zeta}^{m}(r_{p,q})(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))
with
M=m
,
L=0
.

By (
5.60
), (
5.61
) the general term in
(
5.64
) is

={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{(p-M)_{+}}(\zeta t)^{(q-L)_{+}}\prod_{\nu=1}^{L}\left(\zeta^{(1-m_{\nu}-\ell_{\nu})_{+}}t^{1-j_{\nu}}\right)\,.

(5.67)

Since
|\zeta|\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)
,
|\zeta|t\leq{\mathcal{O}}(1)
,
we can drop the positive part subscripts
and see
that the general term in (
5.64
) is

={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{\big(p-M+q-L+\overset{L}{\underset{\nu=1}{\sum}}(1-m_{\nu}-\ell_{\nu})\big)}\,\,t^{\big(q-\overset{L}{\underset{\nu=1}{\sum}}j_{\nu}\big)}={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{p+q-m-\ell}t^{q-j}\,.

By (
5.62
), (
5.64
), we get

\partial_{\zeta}^{m}\partial_{\overline{\zeta}}^{\ell}\partial_{t}^{j}(r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)))={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{3-m-\ell}t^{3-j},\quad t\geq T\,.

(5.68)

In particular,

\partial_{t}(r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))={\mathcal{O}}(1)\zeta^{3}t^{2},\ t\geq T

(5.69)

and more generally,

r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,\cdot))\in\zeta^{3}\mathbb{S}^{3}([0,+\infty[),\ \hbox{ uniformly in }\zeta\,.

(5.70)

Recall that
\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=\mathrm{i}\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))
satisfies (
5.35
),

|\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)|\asymp|\zeta|^{2}t,\ \ t\geq T_{0}\,.

(5.71)

When

\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
is contained in a sufficiently small
neighborhood of
(0,0)
,
we can make
|\zeta|t
as small as we like by
(
5.61
). Then we get
|\zeta|^{3}t^{2}\ll|\zeta|^{2}t
, so

|\partial_{t}(r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)))|\ll|\partial_{t}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)|\,,

(5.72)

implying

|\partial_{t}\varphi(\zeta,t)|\asymp|\zeta|^{2}t,\hbox{ when }t\in[T_{0},+\infty[\hbox{ and }(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\,.

(5.73)

By the same argument,

\partial_{t}\varphi\in|\zeta|^{2}\mathbb{S}^{1}([T_{0},+\infty[),\hbox{ uniformly in
}\zeta

(5.74)

and this and (
5.73
) imply that (
5.35
), (
5.36
)
hold with
\varphi_{2}
replaced by
\varphi
. The proof of Proposition

5.6
then runs with
\varphi_{2}
replaced by
\varphi
. Hence,

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume that
\chi
in (
4.58
) has
sufficiently small support.
For
T\geq T_{0}
,
T\gg\frac{\sqrt{h}}{|\zeta|}
, we have for every
b\in|\zeta|\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
:

\int_{T}^{+\infty}b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi(\sigma,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t=-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{h}\varphi(\sigma,T)}\left(c^{(N)}(T)+\mathcal{O}(1)\left(\frac{h}{(T|\zeta|)^{2}}\right)^{N+1}\frac{h}{T|\zeta|}\right).

(5.75)

Here
c^{(N)}
is defined as in (
5.37
), but with
\varphi_{2}

replaced by
\varphi
, (
5.43
) remains valid.

The integral
\int_{0}^{T}b(t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t
can be computed to wanted precision with standard numerical methods, no
asymptotics expressions are needed for this expression.

5.2
The small
\varepsilon
limit

We now turn to the case when

\varepsilon\leq h^{\delta}
for some small fixed
\delta>0
.
Recall some quantities. We study the integral in (
5.12
),
which can be written

\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

(5.76)

b(\zeta,t;h)=(a\chi)(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\frac{\overline{\widetilde{\zeta}}(\zeta,t)}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}\,,

(5.77)

\varphi(\zeta,t)=\mathrm{i}\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\,.

(5.78)

Here
\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)
is given by (
5.7
),
(
5.56
),

\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)=|\zeta|(2s_{+}(\sigma)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t))=(\mathrm{i}\zeta+\overline{\zeta})t+\overline{\zeta}g_{1}(t)

(5.79)

and
\sigma=\dfrac{\zeta}{|\zeta|}
, with the assumption that

|s_{+}(\sigma)|\geq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
or equivalently that
\sigma
is
not in a conic neighborhood of
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\mathbb{R}
in
{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}
.
g_{1}
is given by (
5.9
),

g_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{t+\sqrt{t^{2}+1}}=\sqrt{t^{2}+1}-t\,.

(5.80)

Recall that
\varepsilon\,=\dfrac{|\zeta|}{\sqrt{h}}
. The first identity in
(\ref{4.sep1})
can be written

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{h}\varepsilon\,\left(2s_{+}(\sigma)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\right)

(5.81)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{h}\varepsilon\,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t)\,.

Let
\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})
be the leading quadratic form part in the Taylor expansion of
\phi
at
(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=(0,0)
,

\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})+r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})\,,

(5.82)

r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})={\mathcal{O}}((\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})^{3})\hbox{ in }\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2})\,.

(5.83)

Recall that

\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=\mathrm{i}\phi_{2}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\,,

(5.84)

where
\phi_{2}
is given by (
5.14
).

The function
\phi_{2}
is positively homogeneous of degree 2 and
\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)
is positively homogeneous of degree 1 with respect to

\zeta
, hence
\varphi(\zeta,t)
is positively homogeneous of
degree 2 with respect to
\zeta
,

\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=|\zeta|^{2}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\,,

(5.85)

or equivalently,

\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\,.

(5.86)

Recall (
5.28
) in an abbreviated form,

\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)=\\
\frac{(\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho)}{8}\Big(-\big(2s_{+}\,t+\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{\lambda-\mu}{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}\sigma\big)^{2}+\mathrm{i}\upsilon(\sigma,t)\Big)\,,

(5.87)

where
\displaystyle\upsilon=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\upsilon_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,+\infty[)
is described in
(
5.20
)–(
5.22
) and
s_{+}
is given by
(
5.8
). Write

\varphi=\mathrm{i}\phi(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\,,

(5.88)

\varphi=\varphi_{2}+\psi,\ \psi(\zeta,t)=\mathrm{i}r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))\,,

(5.89)

r(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=\sum_{k=3}^{\infty}r_{k}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=\sum_{k=3}^{K-1}r_{k}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})+r^{K}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})\,,

(5.90)

in
\mathrm{neigh}((0,0);\mathbb{C}^{2})
, where
r_{k}={\mathcal{O}}((\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})^{k})
is homogeneous of degree
k
,
r^{K}={\cal O}((\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})^{K})
.
Then since
\zeta=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\sigma
,
\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)=h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t)
,

\frac{1}{h}\psi=\mathrm{i}\sum_{3}^{K-1}h^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{k}\underbrace{r_{k}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))}_{=:r_{k}(\sigma,t)}+\mathrm{i}h^{\frac{K}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{K}\widetilde{r}_{K}(\sigma,t;h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)\,,

(5.91)

where

\widetilde{r}_{K}(\sigma,t;h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon):=(\varepsilon h^{\frac{1}{2}})^{-K}r^{K}(\varepsilon h^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma,\varepsilon h^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\,.

(5.92)

Here
r_{k}(\sigma,t)
,
\widetilde{r}_{K}(\sigma,t;h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)
are well defined for

h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\,t={\mathcal{O}}(1)\,,

(5.93)

and belong respectively to

(1+t)^{k}\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,{\mathcal{O}}(1)(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{-1}[)
and
(1+t)^{K}\mathbb{S}^{0}([0,{\mathcal{O}}(1)(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{-1}[)
. In (
5.91
) we have

\begin{split}h^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\varepsilon\,^{k}r_{k}(\sigma,t))&\in h^{-1}h^{\frac{k}{2}}(\varepsilon(1+t))^{k}\mathbb{S}^{0}\,,\\
h^{\frac{K}{2}-1}\varepsilon\,^{K}\widetilde{r}_{K}(\sigma,t;\varepsilon h^{\frac{1}{2}}))&\in h^{-1}h^{\frac{K}{2}}(\varepsilon\,(1+t))^{K}\mathbb{S}^{0}\,.\end{split}

(5.94)

In particular for
K=3

\frac{1}{h}\psi\in h^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varepsilon(1+t))^{3}\mathbb{S}^{0}.

(5.95)

We have
h^{-1}\varphi_{2}\in(\varepsilon(1+t))^{2}\mathbb{S}^{0}

and

\frac{1}{h}\mathrm{Re}\,\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=\varepsilon^{2}\mathrm{Re}\,\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)\asymp-(\varepsilon\,t)^{2},\hbox{ for }t\gg 1.

(5.96)

(
5.95
), (
5.96
) imply that

\frac{1}{h}|\psi|\ll-\frac{1}{h}\mathrm{Re}\,\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)

(5.97)

when
t\gg 1
and
h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon t\ll 1
. The last bound is
equivalent to
|\zeta|t\ll 1
, or:

|\zeta|\ll 1\hbox{ and }|\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t)|\ll 1

which is satisfied for our integrals if
\mathrm{supp}\chi
is
contained in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
(0,0)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
, which we assume from now on. In this region we have

\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}={\mathcal{O}}(1)\mathrm{e}^{-(h^{-2\delta}+(\varepsilon t)^{2})/{\mathcal{O}}(1)},\hbox{ when }\varepsilon t\geq h^{-\delta},

for any fixed
\delta
with
0<\delta\ll 1
. It follows that the
integral (
5.76
) can be decomposed as

\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0\leq\varepsilon t\leq h^{-\delta}}\hskip-20.0ptb(\zeta,t)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\,,

(5.98)

when
b={\mathcal{O}}(1)
.

On the interval
[0,(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}]
we have
\varepsilon t\leq h^{-\delta}
and we choose
\delta\in]0,1/6[
and restrict the attention to this interval until further
notice. (
5.94
), (
5.95
) give for
k,\,K\geq 3
,

\begin{split}h^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{k}r_{k}(\sigma,t)&\in\,h^{k(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1}\,\mathbb{S}^{0},\\
h^{\frac{K}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{K}\widetilde{r}_{K}(\sigma,t;\varepsilon h^{\frac{1}{2}})&\in\,h^{K(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1}\,\mathbb{S}^{0},\\
\frac{1}{h}\psi&\in\,h^{\frac{1}{2}-3\delta}\,\mathbb{S}^{0}\,,\end{split}

where the exponents are positive,

k(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1,\quad K(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)-1,\quad\frac{1}{2}-3\delta>0\,.

We can therefore Taylor expand the exponential function at
0
and get
on
[0,(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}]
(cf. (
5.95
)),

\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\psi}{h}}=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{N!}\left(\frac{\psi}{h}\right)^{n}+{\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{\frac{N}{2}}(\varepsilon(1+t))^{3N}\,.

Here we apply (
5.91
) to each
\psi/h
and see that on

[0,(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}]

\begin{split}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\psi}{h}}=&{\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{\frac{N}{2}}(\varepsilon(1+t))^{\widetilde{N}}+\hbox{a linear combination of products }\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(h^{\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1}\varepsilon\,^{k_{j}}r_{k_{j}}(\sigma,t)\right),\quad\hbox{with }\sum(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)<\frac{N}{2},\end{split}

(5.99)

including the term 1 for
n=0
.
Here
\widetilde{N}=\widetilde{N}(N)
is large enough.
The coefficients in the linear combination are constant and explicitly
computable.
The products in (
5.99
) are

={\mathcal{O}}(1)h^{\overset{n}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)}(\varepsilon(1+t))^{\underset{j=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}k_{j}}\,.

We now return to the integral (
5.76
). First we
shall only use that

b={\mathcal{O}}(1)\,.

(5.100)

From (
5.96
), (
5.98
) we get

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t&={\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon\,}\right),\\
\int_{(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t&={\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\,,\end{split}

(5.101)

and similarly with
\phi
instead of
\phi_{2}
.
More generally, using that
\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon^{2}\frac{t^{2}}{C}}(\varepsilon\,t)^{\widetilde{N}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, for all
C>0
, we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}(\varepsilon\,t)^{\widetilde{N}}\,\mathrm{d}t&=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right),\\
\int_{(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}(\varepsilon\,t)^{\widetilde{N}}\,\mathrm{d}t&={\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\,,\end{split}

(5.102)

and similarly with
\phi
instead of
\phi_{2}
.

Multiply (
5.99
) with
b\mathrm{e}^{\varphi_{2}/h}
, integrate from

0
to
(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}
and apply the above estimates:

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\,\right)h^{\frac{N}{2}}\ +\hbox{ a linear
combination of terms}\\
&\int_{0}^{(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(h^{\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{k_{j}}r_{k_{j}}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\right)\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

with
\sum(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)<\frac{N}{2}.

Here the general integral on the right hand side is equal to

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{idem\,}\,\mathrm{d}t-\int_{(\varepsilon h^{\delta})^{-1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{idem\,}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
&=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{idem\,}\,\mathrm{d}t+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)h^{\underset{j=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)}\mathrm{e}^{-h^{-2\delta}/{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\,,\end{split}

(5.103)

where "idem" denotes the integrand in the general integral in the
right hand side of (
5.103
).

Recall that
\varphi=\varphi_{2}+\psi
and apply (
5.99
) to get

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\,\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\,)h^{\frac{N}{2}}\ +\hbox{ a linear
combination of terms}\\
&\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(h^{\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1}\varepsilon\,^{k_{j}}r_{k_{j}}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\right)\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

(5.104)

with
\sum(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)<\frac{N}{2}.

As in (
5.102
), each integral in the right hand side of (
5.104
) is

{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})h^{\underset{j=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)}
.

Let
\varphi_{2}^{0}
denote the left hand side of (
5.87
)
with
\upsilon
replaced by 0, so that

\varphi_{2}(\sigma,t)=\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)+\widetilde{\upsilon}(\sigma,t),\ \ \widetilde{\upsilon}(\sigma,t):=\frac{\lambda+\mu+\mathrm{i}2\rho}{8}\,\mathrm{i}\upsilon(\sigma,t)

(5.105)

and

\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)=\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)+\varepsilon^{2}\widetilde{\upsilon}(\sigma,t)\,.

(5.106)

Write

\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon^{2}\widetilde{\upsilon}}=\sum_{\nu=0}^{L-1}\frac{1}{\nu!}(\varepsilon^{2}\widetilde{\upsilon})^{\nu}+{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon^{2L})\,,

\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi_{2}(\zeta,t)}=\sum_{\nu=0}^{L-1}\frac{1}{\nu!}(\varepsilon^{2}\widetilde{\upsilon})^{\nu}\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)}+{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon\,^{2L})\,\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)}\,.

Using this in (
5.104
), we get

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})\left(h^{\frac{N}{2}}+\varepsilon^{2L}\right)+\hbox{ a linear
combination}\\
&\hbox{of terms }\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\,\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)}(\widetilde{\upsilon}(\sigma,t)\varepsilon^{2})^{\nu}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(h^{\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{k_{j}}r_{k_{j}}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\right)\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hbox{with }\sum(\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1)<\frac{N}{2},\ \nu<L.\end{split}

(5.107)

Study of the general term in (
5.107
).

For
k\in[3,+\infty[\cap\mathbb{N}
, we have

r_{k}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))=r_{k}\big(\sigma,2s_{+}(\sigma)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big)\,,

(5.108)

where
r_{k}(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})
is holomorphic near
(0,0)
and positively homogeneous of degree
k
. Here
g_{1}\in\mathbb{S}_{cl}^{-1}([0,+\infty[)
,
g_{1}\sim\frac{1}{2t}+\frac{c_{-2}}{t^{2}}+\ldots
,
t\longrightarrow+\infty
and we get

r_{k}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))=\sum_{\nu+\mu=k}r_{k}^{\nu,\mu}\sigma^{\nu}\big(2s_{+}(\sigma)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\big)^{\mu}\in\mathbb{S}^{k}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)\,,

\displaystyle r_{k}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu+\mu+\rho=k}r_{k}^{\nu,\mu,\rho}t^{\mu}g_{1}(t)^{\rho}

(5.109)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{\underset{\nu,\mu,\rho,\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}{\nu+\mu+\rho=k}}r_{k}^{\nu,\mu,\rho,\alpha}t^{\mu-\rho-2\alpha}\,,\qquad t\to+\infty\,,

where we keep in mind that
r_{k}^{\nu,\mu,..}
depend on
\sigma
.
This can be expressed as

r_{k}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\sim\sum_{\mathbb{Z}\ni\mu\leq k}r_{k,\mu}t^{\mu},\qquad t\longrightarrow+\infty\,.

(5.110)

We apply this to (
5.107
) and write

\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Big(h^{\frac{k_{j}}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{k_{j}}r_{k_{j}}(\sigma,\widetilde{\zeta}(\sigma,t))\Big)=h^{\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-n}\varepsilon^{\widetilde{K}}\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{K}}(\sigma,t)\,,

(5.111)

\widetilde{K}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j},\qquad\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{K}}(\sigma,t)\in\mathbb{S}^{\widetilde{K}}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)\,.

(5.112)

In the non-trivial case
n\geq 1
, we have
1\leq n\leq[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]
, where
[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]
denotes the integer part; the largest integer
\leq\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}
.

By (
5.101
), (
5.102
) the contribution from (
5.111
) to
the corresponding term in (
5.107
) is
\mathcal{O}(1/\varepsilon\,)\varepsilon^{2\nu}h^{\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]}
so we can discard the terms with
\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]\geq\frac{N}{2}-1
and only keep the ones with
\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]<\frac{N}{2}-1
.

Recall that
b
is given in (
5.77
), and assume for simplicity
that
a(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})
is independent of
h
,
|\zeta|
is
assumed to be small and
\varepsilon\,t=\mathcal{O}(h^{-\frac{1}{2}})

in the region where
\chi=1
so the exponential factor becomes

\mathcal{O}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-1/\mathcal{O}(h)}\big)
in the cutoff region
where
\varepsilon\,t\asymp h^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For this reason we
simply ignore the cutoff
\chi
.

We have

a(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta})=\sum_{j+k<N}a_{j,k}\zeta^{j}\widetilde{\zeta}^{k}+\mathcal{O}(1)\big(|\zeta|+|\widetilde{\zeta}|\big)^{N},

so

\hskip-5.0pta(\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}(\zeta,t))=\sum_{j+k<N}a_{j,k}\sigma^{j}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j+k}\Big(2s_{+}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{4}}t+\overline{\sigma}g_{1}(t)\Big)^{k}+\mathcal{O}(1)h^{\frac{N}{2}}\Big(\varepsilon(1+t)\Big)^{N}.

(5.113)

The general term belongs to
(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j+k}\mathbb{S}^{k}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)
and is of the form
(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j+k}c_{j,k}(t)
, where
c_{j,k}\in\mathbb{S}^{k}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)
.

Regrouping the terms with the same
j+k
, we can write the double sum
in (
5.113
) as

\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j}c_{j}(t),\qquad c_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{j}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)\,.

(5.114)

From (
5.77
), (
5.79
) it follows that

(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{-1}b(\zeta,t;h)
has the same structure as in (
5.113
), (
5.114
),

\frac{b(\zeta,t)}{h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\,}=\mathcal{O}(1)h^{\frac{N}{2}}\Big(\varepsilon(t+1)\Big)^{N}+\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j}b_{j}(t)\,,

(5.115)

b_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{j}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)\,.

(5.116)

Recall (
5.105
), where
\upsilon={\overset{2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}}\upsilon_{j}
and
(
5.20
)-(
5.22
), to see that
\widetilde{\upsilon}\in\mathbb{S}^{0}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)
. Combine this with (
5.115
),
(
5.116
), (
5.111
). (
5.107
), where now

(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{-1}b={\mathcal{O}}(1)
, gives

\begin{split}\frac{1}{h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon}&\int_{0}^{+\infty}b(\zeta,t;h)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{h}\varphi(\zeta,t)}\mathrm{d}t={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})\left(h^{\frac{N}{2}}+\varepsilon^{2L}\right)+\,\hbox{a linear
combination }\\
&\hbox{ of terms }\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{2}^{0}(\sigma,t)}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N}(h^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon)^{j}b_{j}(t)\Big)\big(\widetilde{\upsilon}(\sigma,t)\varepsilon^{2}\big)^{\nu}h^{\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-n}\varepsilon^{\widetilde{K}}\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{K}}(\sigma,t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

(5.117)

with
\widetilde{K},N,\nu\in\mathbb{N},\nu<L,\frac{\widetilde{K}}{2}-[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]<\frac{N}{2},n\leq[\frac{\widetilde{K}}{3}]
and

b_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{j}_{cl}([0,+\infty[),\quad\widetilde{\upsilon}\in\mathbb{S}^{0}_{cl}([0,+\infty[),\quad\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{K}}(\sigma,t)\in\mathbb{S}^{\widetilde{K}}_{cl}([0,+\infty[)\,.

(5.118)

Analytic singularity at
\varepsilon\,=0
.

Rather than computing the full asymptotics of (
5.117
) when

\varepsilon\to 0
, we settle for terms that are non-analytic there.
Recall that
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}
